Each is the `-ya` absolutive of §597 in the shape the final would give it — `m + y` as `mm` (compare `gammaṃ`, [§544 (556): Vada mada gamu yuja garahākārādīhi jja mma gga yheyyā gāro vā](#m544)), `h + y` reversed as `yh` (`gārayhaṃ`), `d + y` as `jj` (`vajjaṃ`), `bh + y` as `bbh` — stated as ready-made replacements; `ddhā` (`āraddhā`) stands apart, an absolutive with the consonant of the past participle (`āraddho`, [§611 (608): Hacatutthānamantānaṃ do dhe](#m611)). The `vā` opens the option, and the vutti pairs each form with its `-itvā` alternative.

:::

> Ma ha da bha iccevamantehi dhātūhi tunādīnaṃ paccayānaṃ mma yha jja bbha ddhā ādesā honti vā antalopo ca.

> Āgamma, āgamitvā, okkamma. Okkamitvā, paggayha, paggaṇhitvā, uppajja, uppajjitvā, ārabbha, ārabhitvā, āraddhā[^60], ārabhitvā.

Optionally, after roots ending in `m`, `h`, `d` and `bh`[^94] the suffixes `tuna` and the like are replaced by `mma`, `yha`, `jja`, `bbha` and `ddhā` [respectively], and the final [of the root] is elided.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🔽(ā + √gamu + mma) | āgamma | having come to; depending on, owing to |
| 🔽(ā + √gamu + tvā) | āgamitvā | having come to — the option not taken |
| 🔽(ava + √kamu + mma) | okkamma | having stepped aside from, having turned off |
| 🔽(ava + √kamu + tvā) | okkamitvā | having stepped aside — the option not taken |
| 🔽(pa + √gaha + yha) | paggayha | having taken up, having held up |
| 🔽(pa + √gaha + tvā) | paggaṇhitvā | having taken up — the option not taken |
| 🔽(u + √pada + jja) | uppajja | having arisen |
| 🔽(u + √pada + tvā) | uppajjitvā | having arisen — the option not taken |
| 🔽(ā + √rabha + bbha) | ārabbha | having begun; concerning, with reference to |
| 🔽(ā + √rabha + tvā) | ārabhitvā | having begun — the option not taken |
| 🔽(ā + √rabha + ddhā) | āraddhā | having begun |
| 🔽(ā + √rabha + tvā) | ārabhitvā | having begun — the option not taken |

|     | ā + gam + tvā                | rule |
| --- | ---------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ā + gam + (tvā→mma)          | §600 |
| →   | ā + ga + ~~m~~ + mma         | §600 |
| →   | **āgamma**                   |      |

|     | pa + gah + tvā               | rule |
| --- | ---------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pa + gah + (tvā→yha)         | §600 |
| →   | pa + ga + ~~h~~ + yha        | §600 |
| →   | pa + (g→gg) + ayha           | §28  |
| →   | **paggayha**                 |      |

|     | ud + pad + tvā               | rule |
| --- | ---------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ud + pad + (tvā→jja)         | §600 |
| →   | ud + pa + ~~d~~ + jja        | §600 |
| →   | u + (d→p) + pajja            |      |
| →   | **uppajja**                  |      |

|     | ā + rabh + tvā               | rule |
| --- | ---------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ā + rabh + (tvā→bbha)        | §600 |
| →   | ā + ra + ~~bh~~ + bbha       | §600 |
| →   | **ārabbha**                  |      |

The `g` of `gaha` is doubled after the prefix by [§28 (40): Para dvebhāvo ṭhāne](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m28); the `d` of the prefix `ud` takes the shape of the `p` that follows it. In the `-itvā` forms the root keeps its final before the augment `i` of [§605 (547): Yathāgamamikāro](#m605) by [§617 (633): Na ma ka rānamantānaṃ niyuttatamhi](#m617); `paggaṇhitvā` has the verb stem `gaṇhā` of [§450 (517): Gahādito ppa ṇhā](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m450), and `uppajjitvā` the `y` augment of [§606 (642): Dadhantato yo kvaci](#m606).

> `taṃ āgamma assaddhā honti` \
> [(1V 1.2.13: Kuladūsakasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.2/1.2.13#1772) \
🚹②⨀(ta) 🔽(ā + √gamu + mma) 🚹①⨂(assaddha) ▶️🤟⨂(hoti) \
him | owing to | without faith | they become

> `maggā okkamma yena aññataro vanasaṇḍo` \
> [(3V 1.11: Bhaddavaggiyavatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/1/1.11#152) \
🚹⑤⨀(magga) 🔽(ava + √kamu + mma) 🔼(yena) 🚹①⨀(aññatara) 🚹①⨀(vanasaṇḍa) \
from the road | having turned aside | where | a certain | grove [was]

> `samādāya paggayha tiṭṭheyya` \
> [(1V 1.2.10: Saṃghabhedasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.2/1.2.10#1679) \
🔽(saṃ + ā + √dā + ya) 🔽(pa + √gaha + yha) ⏯🤟⨀(tiṭṭhati) \
having taken up | having held up | he should persist

> `latā uppajja tiṭṭhati` \
> [(18Dh 24.2: Sūkarapotikāvatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Dh/24/24.2#366) \
🚺①⨀(latā) 🔽(u + √pada + jja) ▶️🤟⨀(tiṭṭhati) \
the creeper | having sprung up | stands

> `te kulaputte ārabbha bhikkhū āmantesi` \
> [(10M 2.8: Naḷakapānasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/10M/2/2.8#436) \
🚹②⨂(ta) 🚹②⨂(kulaputta) 🔽(ā + √rabha + bbha) 🚹②⨂(bhikkhu) ⏮🤟⨀(āmanteti) \
those | young men | concerning | the bhikkhus | he addressed

> `uppajjitvā nirujjhanti` \
> [(7D 3.36: Parinibbutakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/7D/3/3.36#493) \
🔽(u + √pada + tvā) ▶️🤟⨂(nirujjhati) \
having arisen | they cease

:::

[^60]: CST4 prints `āraddha`; the replacement the sutta prescribes is `ddhā`, and the Padarūpasiddhi (645) reads `āraddhā`.

[^94]: U Nandisena (2005) translates "after the roots ending in ma, ha, da, ma, and others" and gives the fifth replacement as `ddha`. `iccevamantehi` is "ending in these", a closed list of four finals, so "and others" has no warrant, and the stray "ma" and "ddha" are slips; every edition reads `ddhā` in the sutta, and `āraddhā` is its example (Rūpasiddhi 645).

<a name="m601"></a>

### §601 (334): Taddhitasamāsakitakā nāmaṃ vā’ tave tunādīsu ca (Derivatives, compounds and `kita` words count as nouns, except those in `tave`, `tuna` and the like) :badge[saññaṅga]{variant=tip}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        |        | taddhitasamāsakitakā (saddā) | (daṭṭhabbā) | nāmaṃ va | | atave tunādīsu ca |
|        |        | words ending in a `taddhita` suffix, a compound, or a `kita` suffix | (are to be regarded as) | just like a `nāma` | | except those ending in `tave`, `tuna` and the like |

This is a rule about the use of a term, not an operation, and it interrupts the absolutive rules only because `tave` and `tuna` have just been in view. `Taddhitasamāsakitakā` are words that end in a `taddhita` suffix (chapter 5), a compound (`samāsa`, chapter 4) or a `kita` suffix (this chapter). `nāmaṃ vā’ tave` is `nāmaṃ va atave`: `va` is the particle of comparison (`iva`, "just like"), as the vutti's `nāmaṃva daṭṭhabbā` shows, and its `ā` is only the sandhi of `va` with the privative `a` of `a-tave-tunādīsu`, "not in [words ending in] `tave`, `tuna` and the like" — so despite its look the sutta carries no option marker. The point is inflection: the endings `si`, `yo` … of [§55 (63): Si yo, aṃ yo, nā hi, sa naṃ, smā hi, sa naṃ, smiṃ su](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m55) are added to a `liṅga`, a nominal stem ([§53 (61): Liṅgañca nippajjate](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m53), [§54 (62): Tato ca vibhattiyo](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m54)), and a word built with a suffix or by compounding is not a stem in its own right; this rule lets it be treated as one, so that `vāsiṭṭha`, `pattadhamma` and `kumbhakāra` inflect like `purisa`. The infinitives and absolutives in `tave`, `tuṃ`, `tuna`, `tvāna`, `tvā` are excepted: they are uninflected, and take no ending. By saying `ca` the designation reaches further, as Buddhappiya explains (Rūpasiddhi 334): to words ending in the `kicca` suffixes (`bhavitabbaṃ`, `kāriyaṃ`), which [§545 (548): Te kiccā](#m545) and [§546 (562): Aññe kita](#m546) keep apart from `kita`, and to words ending in the feminine suffixes `ā`, `ī`, `inī`. The Rūpasiddhi carries the rule in its compound section (334), which is why its number falls so far out of sequence here; the Bālāvatāra quotes it at the close of its compound chapter (`Taddhitādayo nāmaṃ iva daṭṭhabbā taveppabhutipaccaye vajjetvā`).

:::

> Taddhitasamāsakitakaiccevamantā saddānāmaṃva daṭṭhabbā tave tuna tvāna tvādipaccayante vajjetvā.

> Vāsiṭṭho, pattadhammo, kumbhakāro iccevamādi.

Words ending in a `taddhita` suffix, a compound, or a `kita` suffix are to be regarded just like nouns (`nāma`), leaving aside those that end in the suffixes `tave`, `tuna`, `tvāna`, `tvā` and the like.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(vasiṭṭha + ṇa) | vāsiṭṭho | a descendant of Vasiṭṭha — a `taddhita` word ([§344 (361): Vāṇa’pacce](/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m344)) |
| 🚹①⨀(patta + dhamma) | pattadhammo | one who has attained the Dhamma — a compound |
| 🚹①⨀(kumbha + √kara + ṇa) | kumbhakāro | a potter — a `kita` word ([§524 (561): Dhātuyā kammādimhi ṇo](#m524)) |

|     | kumbha + kar + ṇa (§524)        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kumbha + kar + (~~ṇ~~)a         | §523 |
| →   | kumbha + k + (a→ā) + ra         | §621, §483 |
| →   | kumbhakāra + si (as a `nāma`)   | §601, §54 |
| →   | kumbhakāra + (si→o)             | §104 |
| →   | **kumbhakāro**                  |      |

The excepted words take no such ending: `kātave`, `kātuṃ` ([§595 (637): Tave tunādīsu kā](#m595)), `gantuna`, `gantvā` ([§596 (551): Gama khana hanādīnaṃ tuṃ tabbādīsu na](#m596)) stand as they are.

> `vāsiṭṭho diṭṭho hoti` \
> [(1V 1.2.9: Dutiyaduṭṭhadosasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.2/1.2.9#1644) \
🚹①⨀(vasiṭṭha + ṇa) 🚹①⨀(diṭṭha) ▶️🤟⨀(hoti) \
Vāsiṭṭha | seen | has been

> `diṭṭhadhammo pattadhammo viditadhammo` \
> [(3V 1.6: Pañcavaggiyakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/1/1.6#85) \
🚹①⨀(diṭṭha + dhamma) 🚹①⨀(patta + dhamma) 🚹①⨀(vidita + dhamma) \
who has seen the Dhamma | who has attained the Dhamma | who has understood the Dhamma

> `so kumbhakāro bhikkhūnaṃ bahū patte karonto` \
> [(1V 1.4.3.2: Ūnapañcabandhanasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.4/1.4.3/1.4.3.2#2273) \
🚹①⨀(ta) 🚹①⨀(kumbha + √kara + ṇa) 🚹④⨂(bhikkhu) 🚹②⨂(bahu) 🚹②⨂(patta) 🚹①⨀(karonta) \
that | potter | for the bhikkhus | many | bowls | making

:::

<a name="m602"></a>

### §602 (6): Dumhi garu (The syllable before a conjunct is `garu`, heavy) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        |        | (yo pubbo akkharo) | (daṭṭhabbo) | garu | dumhi (akkhare) | |
|        |        | (the preceding syllable) | (is to be regarded as) | heavy | before a `du`, a conjunct | |

A definition, the first of two on the weight of syllables. `du` is "a pair" (`dvinnaṃ samūho du`, Rūpasiddhi 6): two or more consonants with no vowel between them, a conjunct (`saṃyoga`); `dumhi akkhare`, "when a conjunct `akkhara` follows". `garu` is the ordinary word for "heavy", so the term says what it means and the badge is `anvattha`. The syllable before a conjunct is heavy whatever its vowel: the short `bhi`, `chi`, `da`, `hu` of the vutti's absolutives all count as heavy before the `tv`. The rule stands here, among the primary derivatives, because the absolutives just derived shorten a long root vowel before `tvā`: `dā` gives `datvā`, `hū` gives `hutvā`, and `bhidi`, `chidi` the grammarians' `bhitvā`, `chitvā` (the canon prefers `bhetvā`, `chetvā`). The shortening is that of [§26 (38): Rassaṃ](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m26), whose commentators count the position before a conjunct among the places where it happens; since the syllable is heavy by position anyway, the word loses nothing in weight. In the Padarūpasiddhi this rule and the next stand among the definitions of letters, right after `Aññe dīghā` (5, 6, 7), and the Bālāvatāra (¶45) states the whole doctrine in a line — `dīgho, saṃyogādipubbo rasso ca garu, lahu tu rasso`: "a long vowel, and a short one before the start of a conjunct, are heavy; a plain short one is light".

:::

> Dumhi akkhare yo pubbo akkharo, so garukova daṭṭhabbo.

> Bhitvā, chitvā, datvā, hutvā.

The `akkhara` (syllable) that stands before a `du` — a conjunct `akkhara` — is to be regarded as `garu` (heavy).

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🔽(√bhidi + tvā) | bhitvā | having broken — `bhi` heavy before `tv` |
| 🔽(√chidi + tvā) | chitvā | having cut — `chi` heavy before `tv` |
| 🔽(√dā + tvā) | datvā | having given — `da` heavy before `tv` |
| 🔽(√hū + tvā) | hutvā | having been — `hu` heavy before `tv` |

|     | dā + tvā            | rule |
| --- | ------------------- | ---- |
| →   | d + (ā→a) + tvā     | §26  |
| →   | **datvā**           |      |

|     | hū + tvā            | rule |
| --- | ------------------- | ---- |
| →   | h + (ū→u) + tvā     | §26  |
| →   | **hutvā**           |      |

`bhitvā` and `chitvā` are not attested in the Tipiṭaka, which has `bhetvā` and `chetvā`, with the `e` of the strengthened root.

> `bhogaṃ datvā vāseti` \
> [(1V 1.2.5: Sañcarittasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.2/1.2.5#1265) \
🚹②⨀(bhoga) 🔽(√dā + tvā) ▶️🤟⨀(vāseti) \
wealth | having given | he keeps [her]

> `heṭṭhā hutvā silaṃ uccāresi` \
> [(1V 1.1.3.2: Vinītavatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.3/1.1.3.2#469) \
🔼(heṭṭhā) 🔽(√hū + tvā) 🚺②⨀(silā) ⏮🤟⨀(uccāreti) \
below | being | a stone | he lifted

> `chetvā khīlaṃ chetvā palighaṃ` \
> [(7D 7: Mahāsamayasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/7D/7#752) \
🔽(√chidi + tvā) 🚹②⨀(khīla) 🔽(√chidi + tvā) 🚹②⨀(paligha) \
having cut | the stake | having cut | the bar

:::
